# Phase 1b — Desktop App (Tauri 2 + Svelte 5 + Web Audio streamer) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** The kara-always-oki desktop app on the author's Apple Silicon Mac: a library you can search, fill (drop a file, paste a link) and manage; a queue; a floating player bar with the singer slider, key and lyrics timing; and a full-screen karaoke view with synced, word-by-word lyrics, countdowns and duet parts — playing the engine's separated audio live through Web Audio.

**Architecture:** Part A finishes the `kara-core` API the app needs (add-only job, link preview, lyric voice tags, library CRUD, automatic artwork, instrumental detection, storage, and stable problem codes for user-visible errors). Part B adds `app/`: a Tauri 2 shell (`app/src-tauri`, commands split per module, engine events forwarded as Tauri events, chunk audio sent as raw bytes through `tauri::ipc::Response`) and a Svelte 5 + SvelteKit (adapter-static) UI whose streamer fetches chunk PCM ahead of the playhead, schedules it gaplessly on the `AudioContext` clock, mixes vocals with a gain, and shifts key with signalsmith-stretch in an AudioWorklet. The play queue and the singer/key/lyrics-timing settings live in Rust app state and are broadcast as one `player` snapshot, so the Phase 2 phone page can mirror them. All UI text is keyed in six languages; in-app updates follow OpenEnlarge.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2021, kara-core (Phase 1a), Tauri 2 (`protocol-asset`), tauri-plugin-updater / -process 2, sys-locale 0.3, objc2-app-kit 0.3; Svelte 5 (runes), SvelteKit 2 + adapter-static, Vite 6, TypeScript 5.6, vitest 4, phosphor-svelte 3.1, signalsmith-stretch 1.3.2, @fontsource Geist / Geist Mono / Bricolage Grotesque.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-kara-always-oki-design.md` (§2 experience, §3.3 streamer). UI reference: `docs/prototype/hifi.html` (approved hi-fi prototype — match it).

**Scope note:**
- Hidden until later phases: Apple Music / Spotify (Phase 3) and phone mics (Phase 2). No provider switcher or provider logos, no Sources sheet or Connect prompts, no mic pill and no phone page. Keep component seams (sidebar top slot, karaoke top-right slot) but render nothing there.
- **Storage** is original file + vocals-only chunks (instrumental = original − vocals, computed by the engine; vocals are `NNNN.flac`, 16-bit at half level). The streamer depends **only** on `cache::track_chunk_pcm`, whose output (vocals + instrumental interleaved f32 PCM at the chunk's exact length) does not change with storage details. Code in Part A is written against the engine after fix round 3 (acbf4fc) and the versioned upgrade steps (eb14e55, `STEPS` + `init(conn, steps)` + newer-version refusal); engine fix round 5 (user-visible strings say KaraAlwaysOK, a "not allowed to read this file" case) must land before Task 8, which bases the problem codes on the real strings: `Worker::spawn(ctx, lock, …) -> Result`, `LyricsLookup::spawn -> Result`, `cache::chunk_pcm(store, lib, …)` (may delete an undecodable original; the next prepare fetches again), `Store::original_path/original_dest`, `store::copy_atomic`, `ChunkOut { index, vocals }`, `Stereo::silence/slice/append` test-only, no `Stem`, no `SeparationRow.size_bytes`. The app runs `cache::startup_cleanup` at launch (Task 11), which also removes old-layout chunk files.
- Songs flagged already-instrumental go through separation before playing like any other (their singer slider just starts at original).
- Not built: the prototype's "Replay first-run" (spec: no way to replay it), its "Prototype only" settings, the phone preview, streaming-only menu items (Audio version), and artwork editing (spec: artwork is never editable).

## Global Constraints

- Before any `cargo` command in this environment run: `source "$HOME/.cargo/env"`.
- Target: the author's MacBook (Apple Silicon, macOS). The app must build and run there with `npm run tauri:dev` from `app/` (`tauri dev --no-watch`: the UI hot-reloads, Rust does not rebuild on file changes — after Rust edits stop and rerun it). Workspace dev profile: `opt-level = 1` for our crates, `3` for dependencies.
- `kara-core` must not depend on Tauri. The app crate `app/src-tauri` is a workspace member (shares `target/` and the workspace profiles).
- Tauri commands live in modules by area (`engine.rs`, `library.rs`, `adding.rs`, `player.rs`, `settings.rs`), never one big file. Command bodies are thin: logic sits in plain functions that take `&Library`/`&Store` and are unit-tested.
- Chunk audio crosses IPC only as raw bytes (`tauri::ipc::Response`), never JSON or base64. Layout: vocals interleaved f32 LE, then instrumental interleaved f32 LE, equal halves.
- The app holds `store.lock()` for its whole lifetime and runs `cache::startup_cleanup` once at launch.
- ONNX Runtime and the model download on first run, shown by the "Getting ready" screen; yt-dlp downloads on first link use (preview or add).
- The CoreML teardown message must never reach the user: silence stdout/stderr on `RunEvent::Exit`.
- Icons: Phosphor via `phosphor-svelte`, **Bold** weight everywhere (set once with `IconContext`); transport buttons (previous / play / pause / next) use `weight="fill"`. Import each icon from `phosphor-svelte/lib/<Name>Icon`. Not Lucide.
- Design tokens live in one file, `app/src/styles/tokens.css`, copied from spec §2's table (Walkman light/dark), fonts Geist (body), Bricolage Grotesque 800 −0.02em (display), Geist Mono (captions, numbers); Comfortable spacing 4/8/12/16/20/24/32/40, radius 8/14, rows 52 px. Every color comes from a token.
- Light/dark follows the system live (`prefers-color-scheme`); no in-app toggle. The karaoke view is always dark (class `dk`) and uses the dark accent.
- Glass rule: everything floating over content is glass (`.glass`: translucent, backdrop blur, hairline edge, no heavy shadow): player bar and its buttons, karaoke top controls, sticky search box, menus, queue panel, toasts, tooltips. Sidebar, tabs and sheets are not glass. Reduced transparency → opaque fill.
- Motion rule: every show/hide and state change animates with only a fade or a short slide+fade from the direction the element comes from; duration 240 ms, easing `cubic-bezier(.2,.7,.2,1)` everywhere (`app/src/lib/motion.ts`, CSS `--t` / `--ease`). Reduced motion → fade only.
- Selection = flat tinted overlay, no shadow.
- Icon-first: every label gets an icon except song title/artist/album; icon-only buttons get a tooltip; no status the user can't act on (no Ready/Preparing badges in lists).
- Copy: plain language, no engineering words in the UI (no "stems", "chunks", "FLAC", "LRCLIB", "ONNX", "yt-dlp", IPs, ports). Singer slider tooltip/aria text: "Singer: original" / "Singer: removed" / "Singer: N% removed".
- Code bar (user's): comments only summarize what a function does — no reasoning, history or postmortems; a variable gets a comment only if someone would reasonably ask. Code explains itself through names and structure. Less code and reuse over new layers (YAGNI). Every test must earn its place; no redundant tests.
- Probe over reasoning: when an API or behavior is in doubt, run a focused command, test or read the real source before deciding.
- Any sample lyrics in code or tests are made up. Never real song lyrics.
- Frontend tests: vitest for pure logic only (`*.test.ts` next to the module). Rust: `cargo test --workspace`. Type check: `npm run check` (svelte-check) must report 0 errors.
- Display name **KaraAlwaysOK** (product name, window title, menu bar, About row). Repo, crates, the `kara` CLI and the data folder `~/Library/Application Support/kara-always-oki` keep their names.
- Languages: English, Japanese, Korean, Simplified Chinese, Traditional Chinese, Spanish. Components hold no UI text: every label, tooltip, aria text, toast and message goes through `t(key, params)` (errors from Rust through `say()`, which reads their problem code — English text is never matched), and a task that adds text adds its keys to all six files in `app/src/lib/i18n/` (each locale is a `Record<Key, string>` literal, so a missing or extra key is a type error). The app follows the system language (English otherwise); Settings can override it. Song titles, artists, albums, playlist names and lyrics show exactly as stored — never translated, romanized or converted between scripts. CJK falls back to PingFang / Hiragino Sans / Apple SD Gothic Neo; layouts must survive longer Spanish text.
- Updates: the updater's `pubkey` and the endpoint's repo owner stay placeholders pointing at README "Before the first release"; never generate, print or commit a signing key.
- No remote actions: no step runs `git push`, `gh`, or pushes a tag.
- Window: the standard system title bar (no transparent, overlay or hidden title). The macOS menu bar uses Tauri's default predefined items only.
- Errors: every user-visible error carries a stable problem code from the engine (`kara_core::problem::Problem`, Task 8); commands fail with `AppError { problem, message }` and the UI maps the code to `problem.<code>` keys. A library made by a newer version is never wrapped: its own code reaches the user.
- Artwork: a song shows its own picture (embedded cover or link thumbnail), else the gradient from a random seed picked once when it was added; a playlist's cover is a 2×2 of its first four songs' pictures, or the first song's picture with fewer than four. Never editable.
- Verification: no task needs computer use. A task's own check is commands only: cargo tests, vitest (component and store tests with `@tauri-apps/api/mocks` `mockIPC` where useful), `npm run check`, and the **app check** — `source "$HOME/.cargo/env" && cargo build -p kara-app`, then `cd app && npm run app-check -- ../.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-27-phase1b-app/shots/task-NN.png` (Task 9; Bash timeout 3 minutes), which starts the built app with `tauri:dev`, waits for its page, saves a picture of the window, quits it and everything it started, and fails if the webview or Rust reported an error or no picture was saved (the log sits next to the picture). It finishes within about 90 s. The task's report names the picture for the reviewer to look at. Anything that needs clicks, typing, ears or system settings goes to the user acceptance checklist at the end of Task 27, which never blocks a task.

## Review Focus

1. **A song shorter than one chunk, and the short last chunk of every song** — the streamer must start once the only chunk is ready and end at the song's real length, not at a whole 10 s boundary. Test: Task 14 `a_song_shorter_than_one_chunk_can_start_and_ends_on_time`.
2. **Seeking at or past the prepared edge while separation is still running** — the seek lands just inside what's ready and the user is told, never a silent jump into missing audio. Test: Task 14 `seeking_past_the_prepared_part_lands_inside_it`.
3. **Deleting a song that is playing or queued** — the queue drops it, the next song takes its place (or the queue ends), and the worker stops preparing it. Test: Task 13 `deleting_the_playing_song_moves_on_to_the_next`.
4. **Pasting a link and then typing before the preview arrives** (or pasting a second link) — a late preview for the old link must not appear. Test: Task 21 `a_late_preview_for_an_older_link_is_ignored`.
5. **Lyrics that start after a long intro, or have a long break, or come with no lines** — a 3-dot countdown before the first line and before each break's next line; breaks are measured from the previous line's last sung word, because LRC lines run until the next line; an empty lyrics list shows "No lyrics found". Test: Task 18 `countdowns_before_the_first_line_and_after_long_breaks`.
6. **Several chunk reads failing at once, then Try again** — every failed chunk leaves `fetching` and is read again once the song is prepared again; `onFailed` runs once. Test: Task 15 `reads every chunk again after two reads failed at once and the song was prepared again`.
7. **Every user-visible error reaches the user in their language** — each engine sentence carries a `Problem` code that exists in `ProblemCode` and all six locales; an unknown code shows a generic message instead of throwing. Tests: Task 8 `user_visible_errors_all_come_from_problem`, Task 10 `says a problem code it doesn't know as a generic message instead of failing`.

## IPC contract (summary; each task's Interfaces block is authoritative)

| Command | Args | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `system_locale` | — | `string \| null` |
| `startup_problem` | — | `AppError \| null` (tries opening the library again first) |
| `setup_engine` | `onProgress: Channel<SetupProgress>` | `()` |
| `list_collections` / `open_collection` | `kind` / `id` | `CollectionCard[]` / `CollectionPage` |
| `get_track` / `track_lyrics` | `trackId` | `Track` / `Lyrics` |
| `search` | `input` | `SearchOutcome` |
| `link_preview` | `url, onUpdate: Channel<LinkPreview>` | `()` |
| `add_file` / `add_link` | `path` / `url` | `Track` |
| `start_adding` | `trackId` | `boolean` (false = already has its audio or is queued; nothing started) |
| `delete_track` | `trackId` | `()` |
| `create_playlist` | `name, trackIds` | `number` |
| `rename_playlist` / `delete_playlist` | `id, name` / `id` | `()` |
| `add_to_playlist` | `playlistId, trackId` | `boolean` (false = already there) |
| `playlists_with` | `trackId` | `number[]` |
| `remove_from_playlist` / `move_in_playlist` | `playlistId, trackId` / `+ to` | `()` |
| `edit_track` | `trackId, title, artist, album` | `Track` |
| `player_state`, `song_ended`, `retry_prepare` | — | `PlayerSnapshot` (`retry_prepare`: `()`) |
| `queue_add` / `play_tracks` / `skip` | `trackId, next` / `trackIds, start` / `delta` | `PlayerSnapshot` |
| `queue_move` / `queue_remove` | `key, to` / `key` | `PlayerSnapshot` |
| `set_singer` / `set_key` / `set_lyric_offset` | `value` / `semitones` / `ms` | `PlayerSnapshot` |
| `playback_info` | `trackId` | `PlaybackInfo` |
| `chunk_pcm` | `trackId, index` | raw bytes (`ArrayBuffer`) |
| `storage_info` / `set_storage_limit` / `clear_storage` | — / `bytes` / — | `StorageInfo` / `()` / `StorageInfo` |
| `reduce_transparency` | — | `boolean` |

Every command fails with `AppError { problem: ProblemCode | null, message }`. Events: `engine` → `EngineEvent` (kara-core `jobs::Event` as JSON, tag `kind`; `failed` carries `problem`), `player` → `PlayerSnapshot`. Plugins: updater (`check`), process (`relaunch`).

# Part A — Engine API completion (`kara-core`)

### Task 1: Serializable library types, schema v2 and already-instrumental songs

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/library/mod.rs` (str_enum macro, `Track`, `TRACK_COLS`, `track_row`, `STEPS`, new `mark_instrumental`, tests)
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/ingest/mod.rs` (`looks_instrumental`, `add_file`, `fetch_audio`, tests)
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/ingest/ytdlp.rs` (`Info.tags`, `Fetched.tags`, print template, test)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Phase 1a `Library`, `ingest::add_file`, `ingest::fetch_audio`, `ytdlp::download`.
- Produces:
  - Every `str_enum!` type (`ProviderId`, `CollectionKind`, `SourceKind`, `SourceStatus`, `SepStatus`, `LyricsSource`) implements `serde::Serialize` as its lowercase string (`"local"`, `"playlist"`, `"lrclib"`, …).
  - `Track` derives `Serialize` with camelCase keys and gains `pub instrumental: bool`. JSON: `{ id, provider, title, artist, album, durationMs, vocalRemoval, keySemitones, instrumental }` (Task 3 adds `artworkPath`).
  - `Library::mark_instrumental(&self, id: i64) -> Result<()>` — sets `instrumental = 1` and `vocal_removal = 0` only when the flag was not set yet, so a slider value the user saves later wins.
  - `ingest::looks_instrumental(texts: &[&str]) -> bool`
  - `ytdlp::Fetched { path, title, artist, album, tags: Vec<String> }` (Task 3 adds `thumbnail`).
  - Adds upgrade step 2 to the engine's existing `STEPS` (the `init(conn, steps)` mechanism and its newer-version refusal stay as they are); `user_version` = 2 after this task.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

In `crates/kara-core/src/library/mod.rs` `mod tests`, add:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn instrumental_songs_start_at_original_and_keep_later_changes() {
        let l = lib();
        let id = l.add_track(&local("Rooftop Static (Instrumental)", None)).unwrap();
        l.mark_instrumental(id).unwrap();
        let t = l.track(id).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((t.instrumental, t.vocal_removal), (true, 0));
        l.set_track_settings(id, 60, 0).unwrap();
        l.mark_instrumental(id).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(l.track(id).unwrap().vocal_removal, 60);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_version_1_database_gains_the_instrumental_flag() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let p = dir.path().join("kara.db");
        let conn = Connection::open(&p).unwrap();
        conn.execute_batch(include_str!("schema.sql")).unwrap();
        conn.pragma_update(None, "user_version", 1).unwrap();
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO track (provider, title, added_at) VALUES ('local', 'Old Song', 0)", []).unwrap();
        drop(conn);
        let l = Library::open(&p).unwrap();
        assert!(!l.search("old", 1).unwrap()[0].instrumental);
    }

    #[test]
    fn tracks_serialize_for_the_app() {
        let l = lib();
        let id = l.add_track(&local("Paper Satellites", Some("Mina Okada"))).unwrap();
        let json = serde_json::to_value(l.track(id).unwrap()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(json["provider"], "local");
        assert_eq!(json["vocalRemoval"], 100);
        assert_eq!(json["durationMs"], 1000);
    }
```

Replace the `Track` struct, `TRACK_COLS` and `track_row`:

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, serde::Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct Track {
    pub id: i64,
    pub provider: ProviderId,
    pub title: String,
    pub artist: Option<String>,
    pub album: Option<String>,
    pub duration_ms: Option<i64>,
    pub vocal_removal: u8,
    pub key_semitones: i8,
    pub instrumental: bool,
}

const TRACK_COLS: &str = "t.id, t.provider, t.title, t.artist, t.album, t.duration_ms, t.vocal_removal, t.key_semitones, t.instrumental";

fn track_row(r: &Row) -> rusqlite::Result<Track> {
    Ok(Track {
        id: r.get(0)?,
        provider: r.get(1)?,
        title: r.get(2)?,
        artist: r.get(3)?,
        album: r.get(4)?,
        duration_ms: r.get(5)?,
        vocal_removal: r.get(6)?,
        key_semitones: r.get(7)?,
        instrumental: r.get(8)?,
    })
}
```

Append the upgrade step to `STEPS`:

```rust
const STEPS: &[Step] = &[
    |tx| Ok(tx.execute_batch(include_str!("schema.sql"))?),
    |tx| Ok(tx.execute_batch("ALTER TABLE track ADD COLUMN instrumental INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0")?),
```

```rust
const INSTRUMENTAL_WORDS: &[&str] = &["instrumental", "karaoke", "offvocal", "伴奏", "カラオケ"];

/// Whether a song's title, album or tags say it is already instrumental.
pub fn looks_instrumental(texts: &[&str]) -> bool {
    texts.iter().any(|t| {
        let t = t.to_lowercase();
        let squashed: String = t.chars().filter(|c| !matches!(c, ' ' | '-' | '_')).collect();
        INSTRUMENTAL_WORDS.iter().any(|w| squashed.contains(w)) || t.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric()).any(|w| w == "inst")
    })
}
```

### Task 2: Library CRUD — user playlists, edit info, delete a song

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/library/mod.rs` (`CollectionRow`, `collections`, new methods, tests)
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/ingest/mod.rs` (`edit_info`, `link_collections` puts new songs first in Imported, test)
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/cache.rs` (`delete_track`, test)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1 (`Serialize` on enums).
- Produces:
  - `CollectionRow { id, provider, kind, name, subtitle, user: bool }` derives `Serialize` (camelCase). `user` = a user-made playlist (`provider_ref` starts with `user:`); Local › Imported has `user = false`.
  - `Library::collection(&self, id: i64) -> Result<CollectionRow>`
  - `Library::create_playlist(&self, name: &str) -> Result<i64>` (Local, kind playlist, `provider_ref = "user:<n>"`)
  - `Library::rename_playlist(&self, id: i64, name: &str) -> Result<()>` and `delete_playlist(&self, id: i64) -> Result<()>` — no-ops for non-user collections
  - `Library::add_to_collection` now returns `Result<bool>` (false = the song was already there); `Library::add_to_front(&self, collection_id, track_id) -> Result<bool>` (same, but at the top — Imported shows the newest song first).
  - `Library::collections_of(&self, track_id: i64) -> Result<Vec<i64>>` (ids of every collection holding the song; the song menu check-marks playlists with it).
  - `Library::remove_from_collection(&self, collection_id: i64, track_id: i64) -> Result<()>`
  - `Library::move_in_collection(&self, collection_id: i64, track_id: i64, to: usize) -> Result<()>` (`to` = index in the list after removal, clamped)
  - `Library::leave_collections(&self, track_id: i64, kind: CollectionKind) -> Result<()>`
  - `Library::prune_empty_collections(&self) -> Result<()>` (albums and artists only)
  - `Library::delete_track(&self, id: i64) -> Result<()>` (cascades to sources, lyrics, collection entries)
  - `ingest::edit_info(lib: &Library, track_id: i64, title: &str, artist: Option<&str>, album: Option<&str>) -> Result<()>`
  - `cache::delete_track(store: &Store, lib: &Library, track_id: i64) -> Result<()>` — removes the song, empty albums/artists, and its audio folder unless another song shares the audio.

`crates/kara-core/src/library/mod.rs` — replace `CollectionRow`, add a row mapper and constant, and update `collections`:

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, serde::Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct CollectionRow {
    pub id: i64,
    pub provider: ProviderId,
    pub kind: CollectionKind,
    pub name: String,
    pub subtitle: Option<String>,
    pub user: bool,
}

const COLLECTION_COLS: &str = "id, provider, kind, name, subtitle, provider_ref LIKE 'user:%'";

fn collection_row(r: &Row) -> rusqlite::Result<CollectionRow> {
    Ok(CollectionRow { id: r.get(0)?, provider: r.get(1)?, kind: r.get(2)?, name: r.get(3)?, subtitle: r.get(4)?, user: r.get(5)? })
}
```

```rust
    /// `provider = None` is the All view.
    pub fn collections(&self, provider: Option<ProviderId>, kind: CollectionKind) -> Result<Vec<CollectionRow>> {
        let sql = format!(
            "SELECT {COLLECTION_COLS} FROM collection WHERE kind = ?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR provider = ?2) ORDER BY name COLLATE NOCASE"
        );
        let mut stmt = self.conn.prepare(&sql)?;
        let rows = stmt.query_map(params![kind, provider], collection_row)?;
        Ok(rows.collect::<rusqlite::Result<_>>()?)
    }

    pub fn collection(&self, id: i64) -> Result<CollectionRow> {
        let sql = format!("SELECT {COLLECTION_COLS} FROM collection WHERE id = ?1");
        self.conn.query_row(&sql, [id], collection_row).with_context(|| format!("collection {id}"))
    }

    /// A new, empty user playlist under Local.
    pub fn create_playlist(&self, name: &str) -> Result<i64> {
        Ok(self.conn.query_row(
            "INSERT INTO collection (provider, kind, provider_ref, name)
             VALUES ('local', 'playlist', 'user:' || (SELECT COALESCE(MAX(id), 0) + 1 FROM collection), ?1) RETURNING id",
            [name],
            |r| r.get(0),
        )?)
    }

    /// Renames a user playlist; other collections are left alone.
    pub fn rename_playlist(&self, id: i64, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
        self.conn.execute("UPDATE collection SET name = ?2 WHERE id = ?1 AND provider_ref LIKE 'user:%'", params![id, name])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Deletes a user playlist; its songs stay in the library.
    pub fn delete_playlist(&self, id: i64) -> Result<()> {
        self.conn.execute("DELETE FROM collection WHERE id = ?1 AND provider_ref LIKE 'user:%'", [id])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Appends at the end; false when the song was already there.
    pub fn add_to_collection(&self, collection_id: i64, track_id: i64) -> Result<bool> {
        Ok(self.conn.execute(
            "INSERT OR IGNORE INTO collection_track (collection_id, track_id, position)
             VALUES (?1, ?2, (SELECT COALESCE(MAX(position) + 1, 0) FROM collection_track WHERE collection_id = ?1))",
            params![collection_id, track_id],
        )? > 0)
    }

    /// Puts a song at the top; false when it was already there.
    pub fn add_to_front(&self, collection_id: i64, track_id: i64) -> Result<bool> {
        Ok(self.conn.execute(
            "INSERT OR IGNORE INTO collection_track (collection_id, track_id, position)
             VALUES (?1, ?2, (SELECT COALESCE(MIN(position) - 1, 0) FROM collection_track WHERE collection_id = ?1))",
            params![collection_id, track_id],
        )? > 0)
    }

    pub fn collections_of(&self, track_id: i64) -> Result<Vec<i64>> {
        let mut stmt = self.conn.prepare("SELECT collection_id FROM collection_track WHERE track_id = ?1")?;
        let rows = stmt.query_map([track_id], |r| r.get(0))?;
        Ok(rows.collect::<rusqlite::Result<_>>()?)
    }

    pub fn remove_from_collection(&self, collection_id: i64, track_id: i64) -> Result<()> {
        self.conn.execute("DELETE FROM collection_track WHERE collection_id = ?1 AND track_id = ?2", [collection_id, track_id])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Moves a song to index `to` of the collection's order.
    pub fn move_in_collection(&self, collection_id: i64, track_id: i64, to: usize) -> Result<()> {
        let mut ids: Vec<i64> = self.collection_tracks(collection_id)?.into_iter().map(|t| t.id).collect();
        let Some(from) = ids.iter().position(|&t| t == track_id) else { return Ok(()) };
        ids.remove(from);
        ids.insert(to.min(ids.len()), track_id);
        let tx = self.conn.unchecked_transaction()?;
        for (pos, t) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
            tx.execute("UPDATE collection_track SET position = ?3 WHERE collection_id = ?1 AND track_id = ?2", params![collection_id, t, pos as i64])?;
        }
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Takes a song out of every collection of `kind`.
    pub fn leave_collections(&self, track_id: i64, kind: CollectionKind) -> Result<()> {
        self.conn.execute(
            "DELETE FROM collection_track WHERE track_id = ?1 AND collection_id IN (SELECT id FROM collection WHERE kind = ?2)",
            params![track_id, kind],
        )?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Deletes albums and artists that no longer hold any song.
    pub fn prune_empty_collections(&self) -> Result<()> {
        self.conn.execute(
            "DELETE FROM collection WHERE kind IN ('album', 'artist') AND id NOT IN (SELECT collection_id FROM collection_track)",
            [],
        )?;
        Ok(())
    }
```

```rust
    pub fn delete_track(&self, id: i64) -> Result<()> {

    }
```

`crates/kara-core/src/ingest/mod.rs` — in `link_collections`, the Imported line becomes `lib.add_to_front(imported, track_id)?;`; after `link_collections`, add:

```rust
/// Saves a local song's title, artist and album, and files it under its new album and artist.
pub fn edit_info(lib: &Library, track_id: i64, title: &str, artist: Option<&str>, album: Option<&str>) -> Result<()> {
    lib.update_track_meta(track_id, title, artist, album)?;
    lib.leave_collections(track_id, CollectionKind::Album)?;
    lib.leave_collections(track_id, CollectionKind::Artist)?;
    link_collections(lib, track_id, artist, album)?;
    lib.prune_empty_collections()
}
```

`crates/kara-core/src/cache.rs` — after `finish`, add:

```rust
/// Removes a song from the library, any album or artist left empty, and its
/// audio folder unless another song shares the same audio.
pub fn delete_track(store: &Store, lib: &Library, track_id: i64) -> Result<()> {
    let hash = lib.selected_source(track_id)?.and_then(|s| s.audio_hash);
    lib.delete_track(track_id)?;
    lib.prune_empty_collections()?;
    let Some(hash) = hash else { return Ok(()) };
    if lib.sources_with_hash(&hash)?.is_empty() {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(store.audio_dir(&hash));
        for r in lib.separations()?.iter().filter(|r| r.audio_hash == hash) {
            lib.delete_separation(&hash, &r.model_id)?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

and replace `collect_tags` with:

```rust
fn collect_tags(rev: &MetadataRevision, out: &mut Tags) {
    if out.picture.is_none() {
        out.picture = rev.visuals().first().map(|v| Picture { data: v.data.to_vec(), media_type: v.media_type.clone() });
    }
    for tag in rev.tags() {
        if tag.key.ends_with("iTunSMPB") {
            out.gapless = out.gapless.or(parse_itunsmpb(&tag.value.to_string()));
            continue;
        }
        let slot = match tag.std_key {
            Some(StandardTagKey::TrackTitle) => &mut out.title,
            Some(StandardTagKey::Artist) => &mut out.artist,
            Some(StandardTagKey::Album) => &mut out.album,
            Some(StandardTagKey::Lyrics) => &mut out.lyrics,
            _ => continue,
        };
        let value = tag.value.to_string();
        if slot.is_none() && !value.trim().is_empty() {
            *slot = Some(value.trim().to_string());
        }
    }
}
```

```rust
/// The file extension to store an image under, from a media type or a file extension.
pub fn image_ext(kind: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
    match kind.to_ascii_lowercase().trim_start_matches("image/") {
        "jpeg" | "jpg" => Some("jpg"),
        "png" => Some("png"),
        "webp" => Some("webp"),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Stores cover art once per distinct image and points the song at it.
pub fn save_artwork(lib: &Library, store: &Store, track_id: i64, bytes: &[u8], ext: &str) -> Result<()> {
    let path = store.artwork_dir().join(format!("{}.{ext}", crate::assets::sha256_hex(bytes)));
    if !path.exists() {
        write_atomic(&path, bytes)?;
    }
    lib.set_artwork(track_id, &path.display().to_string())
}

fn download_artwork(lib: &Library, store: &Store, track_id: i64, url: &str) -> Result<()> {
    let ext = Url::parse(url)?.path().rsplit('.').next().and_then(image_ext).unwrap_or("jpg");
    let bytes = reqwest::blocking::get(url)?.error_for_status()?.bytes()?;
    save_artwork(lib, store, track_id, &bytes, ext)
}
```

### Task 4: Lyric voice tags (duet parts)

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/lyrics.rs` (`Voice`, `Line.voice`, `split_voice`, `parse_lrc`, tests)
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/library/mod.rs` (test literal gains `voice: None`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces:
  - `lyrics::Voice` — serializes as `"m"`, `"f"`, `"both"`.
  - `Line { start_ms, end_ms, text, words, voice: Option<Voice> }`; `voice` is omitted from JSON when `None` and defaults to `None` when reading older stored lyrics. `Line`/`Word` keep **snake_case** JSON keys (`start_ms`, `end_ms`) because they are stored as JSON in the database.
  - Markers recognized at the start of a line's text, case-insensitive, followed by `:` — male: `M`, `Male`, `v1`; female: `F`, `Female`, `v2`; together: `D`, `Duet`, `Both`, `All`, `v1000`. The marker is removed from the text and the voice carries over to later lines until the next marker.

Add the marker parser above `parse_lrc`:

```rust
/// "M: words" → (Some(Voice::M), "words"); text without a voice marker is returned as is.
fn split_voice(text: &str) -> (Option<Voice>, &str) {
    let Some((tag, rest)) = text.split_once(':') else { return (None, text) };
    let voice = match tag.trim().to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
        "m" | "male" | "v1" => Voice::M,
        "f" | "female" | "v2" => Voice::F,
        "d" | "duet" | "both" | "all" | "v1000" => Voice::Both,
        _ => return (None, text),
    };
    (Some(voice), rest.trim())
}
```

Replace `parse_lrc`:

```rust
/// LRC text → timed lines with evenly spread word timings and duet voices.
/// Metadata tags and blank lines produce no line (a blank line still ends the line before it).
pub fn parse_lrc(text: &str, duration_ms: i64) -> Vec<Line> {
    let mut stamped: Vec<(i64, String, Option<Voice>)> = Vec::new();
    let mut voice = None;
    for raw in strip_bom(text).lines() {
        let mut rest = raw.trim();
        let mut times = Vec::new();
        while let Some(inner) = rest.strip_prefix('[') {
            let Some(end) = inner.find(']') else { break };
            match parse_timestamp(&inner[..end]) {
                Some(t) => times.push(t),
                None if times.is_empty() => break,
                None => {}
            }
            rest = &inner[end + 1..];
        }
        let (marker, words) = split_voice(rest.trim());
        if marker.is_some() {
            voice = marker;
        }
        for t in times {
            stamped.push((t, words.to_string(), voice));
        }
    }
    stamped.sort_by_key(|(t, ..)| *t);
    let mut lines = Vec::new();
    for (i, (start, text, voice)) in stamped.iter().enumerate() {
        if text.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        let end = match stamped.get(i + 1) {
            Some((next, ..)) => *next,
            None => duration_ms.max(start + LAST_LINE_MS),
        };
        lines.push(Line { start_ms: *start, end_ms: end, text: text.clone(), words: spread_words(text, *start, end), voice: *voice });
    }
    lines
}
```

Put above the test module in `preview.rs`:

```rust
//! What a pasted link points to, shown before anything is downloaded.

use super::link::host_is;
use anyhow::{bail, Result};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::path::Path;
use std::process::Command;
use std::time::Duration;
use url::Url;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct LinkPreview {
    pub title: String,
    pub channel: Option<String>,
    pub duration_ms: Option<i64>,
    pub thumbnail: Option<String>,
}

/// The oEmbed endpoint for a link, for sites that offer one.
pub fn oembed_endpoint(url: &Url) -> Option<Url> {
    let host = url.host_str()?.trim_end_matches('.').to_ascii_lowercase();
    let base = if host_is(&host, "youtube.com") || host_is(&host, "youtu.be") {
        "https://www.youtube.com/oembed"
    } else if host_is(&host, "soundcloud.com") {
        "https://soundcloud.com/oembed"
    } else if host_is(&host, "vimeo.com") {
        "https://vimeo.com/api/oembed.json"
    } else {
        return None;
    };
    Url::parse_with_params(base, [("format", "json"), ("url", url.as_str())]).ok()
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct OEmbed {
    title: String,
    author_name: Option<String>,
    thumbnail_url: Option<String>,
    duration: Option<f64>,
}

pub fn parse_oembed(json: &str) -> Result<LinkPreview> {
    let o: OEmbed = serde_json::from_str(json)?;
    Ok(LinkPreview { title: o.title, channel: o.author_name, duration_ms: o.duration.map(|s| (s * 1000.0) as i64), thumbnail: o.thumbnail_url })
}

/// A quick preview from the site's oEmbed endpoint; `None` for sites without one.
pub fn oembed(url: &Url) -> Result<Option<LinkPreview>> {
    let Some(endpoint) = oembed_endpoint(url) else { return Ok(None) };
    let client = reqwest::blocking::Client::builder().timeout(Duration::from_secs(8)).build()?;
    let body = client.get(endpoint).send()?.error_for_status()?.text()?;
    parse_oembed(&body).map(Some)
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct Dump {
    title: Option<String>,
    channel: Option<String>,
    uploader: Option<String>,
    duration: Option<f64>,
    thumbnail: Option<String>,
}

/// A full preview from yt-dlp, without downloading anything.
pub fn probe(bin: &Path, url: &Url) -> Result<LinkPreview> {
    let out = Command::new(bin)
        .args(["--dump-json", "--skip-download", "--no-playlist", "--no-warnings"])
        .arg(url.as_str())
        .output()?;
    if !out.status.success() {
        bail!("Couldn't find a song at this link.");
    }
    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout);
    let d: Dump = serde_json::from_str(stdout.lines().next().unwrap_or(""))?;
    Ok(LinkPreview {
        title: d.title.unwrap_or_else(|| "Unknown song".into()),
        channel: d.channel.or(d.uploader),
        duration_ms: d.duration.map(|s| (s * 1000.0).round() as i64),
        thumbnail: d.thumbnail,
    })
}

/// A preview for a direct link to an audio file, from its name.
pub fn file_preview(url: &Url) -> LinkPreview {
    let name = url.path_segments().and_then(|mut s| s.next_back()).unwrap_or("");
    LinkPreview {
        title: super::title_from_file_name(name),
        channel: url.host_str().map(|h| h.trim_start_matches("www.").to_string()),
        duration_ms: None,
        thumbnail: None,
    }
}
```

### Task 6: Add-only job, the Adder thread, and events the app can read

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/kara-core/src/jobs.rs` (`Stage`/`Event` derive `Serialize`, `Stage::FindingLyrics`, `Event::Added`, `fail`, `add`, `add_inner`, `Adder`, tests)
- Modify: `crates/kara-cli/src/main.rs` (`prepare`'s `show` matches `Event` and `Stage` exhaustively)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `load_or_fetch`, `is_ready`, `refresh_lyrics` (Phase 1a).
- Produces:
  - `Event` serializes with tag `kind` and camelCase fields; new variant `Event::Added { track_id }` (JSON `{"kind":"added","trackId":…}`). `Stage` serializes as `"fetching"`, `"standardizing"`, `"findingLyrics"`, `"separating"`; `FindingLyrics` is new and only the add job emits it.
  - `jobs::add(ctx: &Ctx, lib: &Library, fetcher: &dyn LyricsFetcher, track_id: i64, emit: &mut dyn FnMut(Event)) -> Result<()>` — fetch + standardize (skipped when the song is already separated), then looks up lyrics itself (a failed lookup doesn't fail the add; it is tried again when the song is played); emits `Stage` events, `Lyrics` when it saved some, then `Added` — or `Failed` with the source marked failed. Never separates.
  - `jobs::Adder::spawn(ctx: Ctx, fetcher: Box<dyn LyricsFetcher + Send>, events: mpsc::Sender<Event>) -> Result<Adder>` (opens its database connection up front, like `Worker::spawn`); `Adder::add(&self, track_id: i64)`. One background thread, songs one at a time; `Adder` is `Send + Sync`. It does not take the data lock: the app holds that (Task 11).

`crates/kara-core/src/jobs.rs` — derive and extend the event types:

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, serde::Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum Stage {
    Fetching,
    Standardizing,
    FindingLyrics,
    Separating,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, serde::Serialize)]
#[serde(tag = "kind", rename_all = "camelCase", rename_all_fields = "camelCase")]
pub enum Event {
    Stage { track_id: i64, stage: Stage },
    Progress { track_id: i64, chunks_done: u32, chunks_total: u32 },
    /// Lyrics were saved, including "none found"; read them from the library.
    Lyrics { track_id: i64 },
    /// A newly added song's audio and lyrics are ready; it is separated when played.
    Added { track_id: i64 },
    Ready { track_id: i64 },
    Failed { track_id: i64, message: String },
}
```

Add a shared failure helper and use it in `prepare`'s `Err` arm (`Err(e) => fail(lib, track_id, e, emit),`):

```rust
/// Marks the song's audio failed with the error's plain message and reports it.
fn fail(lib: &Library, track_id: i64, e: &anyhow::Error, emit: &mut dyn FnMut(Event)) {
    let message = e.to_string();
    if let Ok(Some(src)) = lib.selected_source(track_id) {
        let _ = lib.set_source_status(src.id, SourceStatus::Failed, Some(&message));
    }
    emit(Event::Failed { track_id, message });
}
```

Add after `prepare`:

```rust
/// Gets a newly added song's audio onto disk in the standard format and looks up
/// its lyrics, without taking the vocals out. Emits `Added` when done.
pub fn add(ctx: &Ctx, lib: &Library, fetcher: &dyn LyricsFetcher, track_id: i64, emit: &mut dyn FnMut(Event)) -> Result<()> {
    let result = add_inner(ctx, lib, fetcher, track_id, emit);
    match &result {
        Ok(()) => emit(Event::Added { track_id }),
        Err(e) => fail(lib, track_id, e, emit),
    }
    result
}

fn add_inner(ctx: &Ctx, lib: &Library, fetcher: &dyn LyricsFetcher, track_id: i64, emit: &mut dyn FnMut(Event)) -> Result<()> {
    let track = lib.track(track_id).context("This song is no longer in your library.")?;
    let source = lib
        .selected_source(track_id)
        .context("This song is no longer in your library.")?
        .context("This song has no audio to play.")?;
    let separated = match &source.audio_hash {
        Some(hash) => is_ready(ctx, lib, hash)?,
        None => false,
    };
    if !separated {
        load_or_fetch(ctx, lib, &track, &source, emit)?;
    }
    emit(Event::Stage { track_id, stage: Stage::FindingLyrics });
    if refresh_lyrics(lib, track_id, fetcher).unwrap_or(false) {
        emit(Event::Lyrics { track_id });
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Adds songs one at a time on its own thread, so a download never waits for
/// or holds up the song being prepared for singing.
pub struct Adder {
    requests: mpsc::Sender<i64>,
}

impl Adder {
    pub fn spawn(ctx: Ctx, fetcher: Box<dyn LyricsFetcher + Send>, events: mpsc::Sender<Event>) -> Result<Self> {
        let lib = Library::open(&ctx.store.db_path())?;
        let (requests, rx) = mpsc::channel::<i64>();
        std::thread::spawn(move || {
            for track_id in rx {
                let _ = add(&ctx, &lib, fetcher.as_ref(), track_id, &mut |e| {
                    let _ = events.send(e);
                });
            }
        });
        Ok(Self { requests })
    }

    pub fn add(&self, track_id: i64) {
        let _ = self.requests.send(track_id);
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`crates/kara-core/src/cache.rs` — rename the body of `enforce_budget` into `evict_to` with a `budget` parameter (the only change inside is `pick_evictions(&entries, budget)`), and add the thin entry points:

```rust
/// Bytes the songs' audio takes on disk.
pub fn usage(store: &Store) -> u64 {
    dir_size(&store.audio_root()) as u64
}

/// Deletes whole songs' audio folders until the cache fits its budget:
/// unfinished songs first, then the least recently played. Never touches
/// `protected` hashes (playing / queued), or a song whose only source is a
/// local file that no longer exists.
pub fn enforce_budget(store: &Store, lib: &Library, protected: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    evict_to(store, lib, protected, budget(lib)?)
}

/// Deletes every song's prepared audio, with the same exceptions as `enforce_budget`.
pub fn clear(store: &Store, lib: &Library, protected: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    evict_to(store, lib, protected, 0)
}

fn evict_to(store: &Store, lib: &Library, protected: &[String], budget: u64) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    let rows = lib.separations()?;
    let hashes: Vec<String> = match std::fs::read_dir(store.audio_root()) {
        Ok(rd) => rd
            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
            .filter(|e| e.file_type().is_ok_and(|t| t.is_dir()))
            .filter_map(|e| e.file_name().into_string().ok())
            .collect(),
        Err(_) => Vec::new(),
    };
    let mut entries = Vec::with_capacity(hashes.len());
    for hash in &hashes {
        let ready = rows.iter().find(|r| &r.audio_hash == hash && r.status == SepStatus::Ready);
        let unrecoverable = lib
            .sources_with_hash(hash)?
            .iter()
            .any(|s| s.kind == SourceKind::File && !Path::new(&s.uri).exists());
        entries.push(Entry {
            size_bytes: dir_size(&store.audio_dir(hash)),
            last_used_at: ready.and_then(|r| r.last_used_at).unwrap_or(0),
            ready: ready.is_some(),
            protected: unrecoverable || protected.contains(hash),
        });
    }
    let mut evicted = Vec::new();
    for i in pick_evictions(&entries, budget) {
        let hash = &hashes[i];
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(store.audio_dir(hash))?;
        for r in rows.iter().filter(|r| &r.audio_hash == hash) {
            lib.delete_separation(hash, &r.model_id)?;
        }
        lib.reset_sources_for_hash(hash)?;
        evicted.push(hash.clone());
    }
    Ok(evicted)
}
```

Create `crates/kara-core/src/quiet.rs`:

```rust
//! Keeping native libraries' shutdown messages off the user's screen.

/// Sends this process's stdout and stderr to /dev/null until it exits.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn silence_output() {
    use std::io::Write;
    use std::os::unix::io::AsRawFd;
    std::io::stdout().flush().ok();
    std::io::stderr().flush().ok();
    if let Ok(devnull) = std::fs::OpenOptions::new().write(true).open("/dev/null") {
        let fd = devnull.as_raw_fd();
        unsafe {
            libc::dup2(fd, libc::STDOUT_FILENO);
            libc::dup2(fd, libc::STDERR_FILENO);
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
pub fn silence_output() {}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::path::Path;

    const SENTENCE_STARTS: &[&str] = &["\"Couldn", "\"Can't", "\"This ", "\"The ", "\"That", "\"Another", "\"Songs", "\"Links", "\"KaraAlwaysOK"];

    fn visit(dir: &Path, f: &mut dyn FnMut(&Path, &str)) {
        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(dir).unwrap() {
            let p = entry.unwrap().path();
            if p.is_dir() {
                visit(&p, f);
            } else if p.extension().is_some_and(|x| x == "rs") {
                f(&p, &std::fs::read_to_string(&p).unwrap());
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn user_visible_errors_all_come_from_problem() {
        let mut left = Vec::new();
        visit(Path::new(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/src")), &mut |path, text| {
            if path.ends_with("problem.rs") {
                return;
            }
            let code = text.split("#[cfg(test)]\nmod ").next().unwrap_or("");
            for (i, line) in code.lines().enumerate() {
                if !line.trim_start().starts_with("//") && SENTENCE_STARTS.iter().any(|s| line.contains(s)) {
                    left.push(format!("{}:{}: {}", path.display(), i + 1, line.trim()));
                }
            }
        });
        assert!(left.is_empty(), "user-visible text without a Problem code:\n{}", left.join("\n"));
    }
}
```

Put above the tests in `crates/kara-core/src/problem.rs`:

```rust
//! User-visible problems, as stable codes the app translates and the CLI prints in English.

use serde::Serialize;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum Problem {
    NewerLibrary,
    UpgradeFailed,
    DataFolder,
    InUse,
    LibraryOpen,
    EngineDownload,
    EngineStart,
    NothingPlaying,
    NotALink,
    FileMoved,
    FileNotAllowed,
    ReadFailed,
    NotAudio,
    SongGone,
    NoAudio,
    Download,
    DownloaderSetup,
    Unreadable,
    SongAudio,
    Empty,
    DiskFull,
    Save,
    Separate,
    NotPrepared,
    PartNotReady,
    StreamingLater,
    LinkStreaming,
    LinkUnsupported,
    NoSongAtLink,
}

impl std::fmt::Display for Problem {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(match self {
            Self::NewerLibrary => "This library was made by a newer version of KaraAlwaysOK. Update KaraAlwaysOK to open it.",
            Self::UpgradeFailed => "This library couldn't be upgraded safely.",
            Self::DataFolder => "Couldn't open KaraAlwaysOK's data folder.",
            Self::InUse => "Another copy of KaraAlwaysOK is using your library. Close it and try again.",
            Self::LibraryOpen => "Couldn't open your library.",
            Self::EngineDownload => "Couldn't download the singing engine. Check your connection.",
            Self::EngineStart => "Couldn't start the singing engine.",
            Self::NothingPlaying => "Nothing is playing.",
            Self::NotALink => "That's not a link.",
            Self::FileMoved => "The file was moved or deleted.",
            Self::FileNotAllowed => "KaraAlwaysOK isn't allowed to read this file.",
            Self::ReadFailed => "Couldn't read this file.",
            Self::NotAudio => "This file isn't audio we can play.",
            Self::SongGone => "This song is no longer in your library.",
            Self::NoAudio => "This song has no audio to play.",
            Self::Download => "Couldn't download this song. Check the link and your connection.",
            Self::DownloaderSetup => "Couldn't set up downloading. Check your connection.",
            Self::Unreadable => "Couldn't read this audio. The format may not be supported.",
            Self::SongAudio => "Couldn't read this song's audio.",
            Self::Empty => "This audio is empty.",
            Self::DiskFull => "Couldn't save the audio. The disk is full.",
            Self::Save => "Couldn't save the audio.",
            Self::Separate => "Couldn't take the vocals out of this song.",
            Self::NotPrepared => "This song isn't prepared yet.",
            Self::PartNotReady => "This part of the song isn't ready yet.",
            Self::StreamingLater => "Songs from streaming libraries arrive in a later version.",
            Self::LinkStreaming => "Links from streaming services can't be downloaded. Search for the song instead.",
            Self::LinkUnsupported => "Can't get audio from this link. Paste a YouTube, SoundCloud or Bandcamp link, or a direct link to an audio file.",
            Self::NoSongAtLink => "Couldn't find a song at this link.",
        })
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for Problem {}

/// The problem an error carries, if any (the outermost one when there are several).
pub fn problem(e: &anyhow::Error) -> Option<Problem> {
    e.downcast_ref::<Problem>().copied()
}
```

Import `crate::problem::Problem` where needed and replace:
- `audio.rs` — `describe_read_failure`:

```rust
/// Explains a failure reading a song file: `unsupported` for a damaged or unrecognized
/// file, that it's missing, that KaraAlwaysOK isn't allowed to read it, or that reading failed.
pub fn describe_read_failure(e: anyhow::Error, unsupported: Problem) -> anyhow::Error {
    match e.downcast_ref::<std::io::Error>().map(std::io::Error::kind) {
        None => e.context(unsupported),
        Some(std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound) => e.context(Problem::FileMoved),
        Some(std::io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied) => e.context(Problem::FileNotAllowed),
        Some(_) => e.context(Problem::ReadFailed),
    }
}
```

  and its tests pass `Problem::NotAudio` where they passed `"unsupported"`; `a_missing_file_is_reported_as_moved_or_deleted` also pins the code:

```rust
/// Why a pasted link can't be used, if it can't.
pub fn rejection(url: &Url) -> Option<Problem> {
    match verdict(url) {
        LinkVerdict::AudioFile | LinkVerdict::Extractable => None,
        LinkVerdict::Streaming => Some(Problem::LinkStreaming),
        LinkVerdict::Unsupported => Some(Problem::LinkUnsupported),
    }
}
```

```rust
        assert_eq!(rejection_of("https://open.spotify.com/track/x"), Some(Problem::LinkStreaming));
        assert_eq!(rejection_of("https://example.com/page"), Some(Problem::LinkUnsupported));
        assert_eq!(rejection_of("https://youtu.be/x"), None);
    }
```

`app/vite.config.js`:

```js
/// <reference types="vitest/config" />
import { defineConfig } from "vite";
import { sveltekit } from "@sveltejs/kit/vite";

const host = process.env.TAURI_DEV_HOST;

/** Prints what the webview posts to /__webview-log (development only; see hooks.client.ts and +layout.svelte). */
const webviewLog = {
  name: "webview-log",
  /** @param {import("vite").ViteDevServer} server */
  configureServer(server) {
    server.middlewares.use("/__webview-log", (req, res) => {
      let body = "";
      req.on("data", (chunk) => (body += chunk));
      req.on("end", () => {
        console.error(body === "ready" ? "[webview-ready]" : `[webview] ${body}`);
        res.end();
      });
    });
  },
};

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [sveltekit(), webviewLog],
  clearScreen: false,
  server: {
    port: 1420,
    strictPort: true,
    host: host || false,
    hmr: host ? { protocol: "ws", host, port: 1421 } : undefined,
    watch: { ignored: ["**/src-tauri/**"] },
  },
  test: { include: ["src/**/*.test.ts"] },
});
```

```css
* { box-sizing: border-box; }
html, body { height: 100%; margin: 0; }
body { background: var(--bg); color: var(--text); font: 14px/1.45 var(--body); -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased; overflow: hidden; user-select: none; -webkit-user-select: none; cursor: default; }
input { user-select: text; -webkit-user-select: text; }
h1, h2, h3, p { margin: 0; }
button, input { font: inherit; color: inherit; }
button { background: none; border: 0; padding: 0; cursor: pointer; transition: background-color var(--t) var(--ease), color var(--t) var(--ease), opacity var(--t) var(--ease), transform var(--t) var(--ease); }
button:disabled { cursor: default; opacity: .5; }

.cap { font: 500 11px/1.3 var(--mono); letter-spacing: .07em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); }
.num { font: 400 12px/1.3 var(--mono); font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; color: var(--muted); }
.muted { color: var(--muted); }
.grow { flex: 1; min-width: 0; }
.ell { white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; }
.hstack { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: var(--s2); }

.btn { display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: var(--s2); height: 32px; padding: 0 var(--s4); border-radius: var(--r-sm); background: var(--raised); font-weight: 500; white-space: nowrap; }
.btn:hover { background: var(--line); }
.btn.accent { background: var(--accent); color: var(--on-accent); }
.btn.accent:hover { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 86%, var(--text)); }
.btn.soft { color: var(--accent); }
.btn.lg { height: 40px; padding: 0 var(--s6); font-size: 15px; font-weight: 600; }
.btn.ghost { background: none; padding: 0 var(--s2); color: var(--muted); }
.btn.ghost:hover { background: var(--raised); color: var(--text); }
.ib { width: 32px; height: 32px; display: grid; place-items: center; border-radius: var(--r-sm); color: var(--muted); flex: none; }
.ib:hover { background: var(--raised); color: var(--text); }
.ib[aria-pressed="true"] { color: var(--accent); background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 14%, transparent); }

.glass { background: var(--glass); color: var(--text); border: 0; -webkit-backdrop-filter: var(--blur); backdrop-filter: var(--blur); box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--glass-edge), 0 0 0 .5px var(--glass-line), 0 10px 30px -20px var(--shadow); }
@media (prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce) {
  .glass { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--surface) 96%, transparent); -webkit-backdrop-filter: none; backdrop-filter: none; }
}
:root[data-reduce-transparency] .glass { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--surface) 96%, transparent); -webkit-backdrop-filter: none; backdrop-filter: none; }

.art { position: relative; overflow: hidden; }
.art::after { content: ""; position: absolute; inset: 0; background-image: url("data:image/svg+xml;utf8,<svg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' width='140' height='140'><filter id='n'><feTurbulence type='fractalNoise' baseFrequency='.9' numOctaves='2' stitchTiles='stitch'/></filter><rect width='100%' height='100%' filter='url(%23n)' opacity='.35'/></svg>"); mix-blend-mode: overlay; opacity: .5; pointer-events: none; }

.spin { width: 14px; height: 14px; flex: none; border-radius: 50%; border: 2px solid var(--busy); border-top-color: transparent; animation: spin .8s linear infinite; }
.bone { display: block; height: 12px; border-radius: 6px; margin: 8px 0; background: linear-gradient(90deg, var(--raised) 30%, var(--line) 50%, var(--raised) 70%); background-size: 300% 100%; animation: shimmer 1.1s linear infinite; }
@keyframes spin { to { transform: rotate(360deg); } }
@keyframes pulse { 50% { opacity: .35; } }
@keyframes shimmer { from { background-position: 100% 0; } to { background-position: 0 0; } }
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .spin, .bone { animation: pulse 1.2s var(--ease) infinite; }
}

.mrow { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: var(--s3); min-height: 40px; padding: 0 var(--s2); }
.mrow > svg:first-child { color: var(--muted); }
.msep { height: 1px; margin: var(--s1) var(--s2); background: var(--line); }
.opt { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: var(--s3); width: 100%; min-height: 40px; padding: var(--s1) var(--s2); border-radius: var(--r-sm); text-align: left; }
.opt:hover { background: var(--raised); }
.opt > svg:first-child { color: var(--muted); }
.stepper { display: flex; align-items: center; height: 28px; border-radius: 999px; background: var(--raised); }
.stepper button { width: 28px; height: 28px; display: grid; place-items: center; border-radius: 50%; }
.stepper button:hover { background: var(--line); }
.stepper output { min-width: 6ch; text-align: center; font: 12px var(--mono); font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; }

.vs { -webkit-appearance: none; appearance: none; width: 110px; height: 4px; margin: 0; border-radius: 4px; cursor: pointer; background: linear-gradient(90deg, var(--text) var(--v, 100%), color-mix(in srgb, var(--text) 18%, transparent) var(--v, 100%)); }
.vs::-webkit-slider-thumb { -webkit-appearance: none; width: 14px; height: 14px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--thumb); box-shadow: 0 0 0 .5px var(--shadow), 0 1px 4px var(--shadow); opacity: 0; transition: opacity var(--t) var(--ease); }
.vs:hover::-webkit-slider-thumb, .vs:focus-visible::-webkit-slider-thumb, .vs:active::-webkit-slider-thumb { opacity: 1; }
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Motion and tooltips**

`app/src/lib/motion.ts`:

```ts
import type { TransitionConfig } from "svelte/transition";

export const DURATION = 240;

/** cubic-bezier(.2, .7, .2, 1), the one easing used everywhere. */
export function ease(x: number): number {
  const at = (a: number, b: number, t: number) => 3 * a * t * (1 - t) ** 2 + 3 * b * t * t * (1 - t) + t ** 3;
  let lo = 0, hi = 1, t = x;
  for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {
    t = (lo + hi) / 2;
    if (at(0.2, 0.2, t) < x) lo = t;
    else hi = t;
  }
  return at(0.7, 1, t);
}

const reducedMotion = () => matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").matches;

export function fade(_node: Element): TransitionConfig {
  return { duration: DURATION, easing: ease, css: (t) => `opacity:${t}` };
}

/** A short slide from (x, y) pixels away, with a fade; only the fade under reduced motion. */
export function slide(node: Element, { x = 0, y = 8 }: { x?: number; y?: number } = {}): TransitionConfig {
  if (reducedMotion()) return fade(node);
  return { duration: DURATION, easing: ease, css: (t, u) => `opacity:${t};transform:translate(${u * x}px,${u * y}px)` };
}
```

`app/src/lib/tooltip.svelte.ts`:

```ts
class TipState {
  current = $state<{ text: string; rect: DOMRect } | null>(null);
}

export const tipState = new TipState();

/** Names an icon-only control: shows `text` as a tooltip after the pointer rests on it. */
export function tip(node: HTMLElement, text: string) {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  const hide = () => {
    clearTimeout(timer);
    tipState.current = null;
  };
  const enter = () => {
    clearTimeout(timer);
    timer = setTimeout(() => (tipState.current = { text, rect: node.getBoundingClientRect() }), 450);
  };
  if (!node.hasAttribute("aria-label")) node.setAttribute("aria-label", text);
  node.addEventListener("pointerenter", enter);
  node.addEventListener("pointerleave", hide);
  node.addEventListener("pointerdown", hide);
  return {
    update(next: string) {
      text = next;
      node.setAttribute("aria-label", next);
    },
    destroy() {
      hide();
      node.removeEventListener("pointerenter", enter);
      node.removeEventListener("pointerleave", hide);
      node.removeEventListener("pointerdown", hide);
    },
  };
}
```

`app/src/lib/components/Tooltip.svelte`:

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import { tipState } from "$lib/tooltip.svelte";
  import { fade } from "$lib/motion";

  let w = $state(0);
  let h = $state(0);
  const pos = $derived.by(() => {
    const r = tipState.current?.rect;
    if (!r) return null;
    const below = r.bottom + 6;
    const top = below + h > innerHeight - 4 ? r.top - h - 6 : below;
    return { top, left: Math.max(4, Math.min(innerWidth - w - 4, r.left + r.width / 2 - w / 2)) };
  });
</script>

{#if tipState.current && pos}
  <div class="tip glass" role="tooltip" style:top="{pos.top}px" style:left="{pos.left}px" bind:offsetWidth={w} bind:offsetHeight={h} transition:fade>
    {tipState.current.text}
  </div>
{/if}

<style>
  .tip { position: fixed; z-index: 70; padding: 4px var(--s2); border-radius: 6px; font-size: 12px; font-weight: 500; white-space: nowrap; pointer-events: none; }
</style>
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The app check**

`app/src/hooks.client.ts`:

```ts
/** In development, sends the webview's errors to the terminal running `tauri dev`, so checks can read them. */
if (import.meta.env.DEV) {
  const send = (kind: string, text: string) => void fetch("/__webview-log", { method: "POST", body: `${kind}: ${text}` }).catch(() => {});
  addEventListener("error", (e) => send("error", String(e.error?.stack ?? e.message)));
  addEventListener("unhandledrejection", (e) => send("unhandled", e.reason instanceof Error ? String(e.reason.stack) : JSON.stringify(e.reason)));
  const error = console.error;
  console.error = (...args: unknown[]) => {
    error(...args);
    send("console.error", args.map(String).join(" "));
  };
}
```

`app/scripts/window-id.swift`:

```swift
// Prints the window number of the first normal on-screen window owned by one of the given processes.
import CoreGraphics

let owners = Set(CommandLine.arguments.dropFirst())
let windows = CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo([.optionOnScreenOnly], kCGNullWindowID) as? [[String: Any]] ?? []
if let w = windows.first(where: { owners.contains($0[kCGWindowOwnerName as String] as? String ?? "") && ($0[kCGWindowLayer as String] as? Int) == 0 }) {
    print(w[kCGWindowNumber as String]!)
}
```

`app/scripts/app-check.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/zsh
# Starts the already-built app with tauri:dev, waits for its page, saves a picture of the window
# to $1, runs $2 (if given) while the app is open, quits it, and fails when the webview or Rust
# reported an error. Finishes within about 90 s; every process it starts is gone when it exits.
set -u
shot=${1:A}
log=${shot:r}.log
cd "${0:A:h}/.."
mkdir -p "${shot:h}"
rm -f "$shot" "${shot:r}.while-open.txt"
fail() { echo "FAIL: $1 Output: $log"; exit 1; }

pkill -x kara-app && sleep 1
[[ -n $(swift scripts/window-id.swift kara-app KaraAlwaysOK) ]] && fail "An old KaraAlwaysOK window is still open."
lsof -ti tcp:1420 >/dev/null && fail "Port 1420 is in use; stop the old dev server (lsof -i tcp:1420)."

perl -MPOSIX -e 'setsid(); exec @ARGV' npm run tauri:dev >"$log" 2>&1 &
dev=$!
stop() {
  kill -- -$dev 2>/dev/null
  pkill -x kara-app
  sleep 2
  kill -9 -- -$dev 2>/dev/null
  pkill -9 -x kara-app
}
trap stop EXIT INT TERM

ready=""
for _ in {1..60}; do
  grep -q '^\[webview-ready\]' "$log" && ready=1 && break
  kill -0 $dev 2>/dev/null || break
  sleep 1
done
[[ -n $ready ]] || fail "The page never loaded within 60 s (build first: cargo build -p kara-app)."
id=$(swift scripts/window-id.swift kara-app KaraAlwaysOK)
[[ -n $id ]] || fail "The page loaded but no app window was found."
sleep 1
caffeinate -u -t 2
screencapture -x -o -l "$id" "$shot"
[[ -n ${2:-} ]] && eval "$2" >"${shot:r}.while-open.txt" 2>&1
stop
trap - EXIT INT TERM

grep -nE '^\[webview\]|panicked|^error' "$log" && fail "Errors above."
[[ -s $shot ]] || fail "No picture was saved (Screen Recording permission, or the display is asleep). No errors were logged."
echo "OK. Picture: $shot"
```

Every task runs it right after `cargo build -p kara-app` (so `tauri dev` only launches, not compiles) and gives the Bash call a 3-minute timeout. `setsid` (through the system `perl`; a script's zsh can't turn on job control) puts the dev tree (npm, tauri, cargo, vite, the app) in its own process group, so `stop` — also run on exit or Ctrl-C — ends all of it and frees port 1420. The log is read only after the app is gone, so errors logged up to the kill count.

Run:
```bash
cd app && npm install && npm run tauri icon ../assets/icon.png && npm run check
cd .. && source "$HOME/.cargo/env" && cargo build -p kara-app
cd app && npm run app-check -- ../.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-27-phase1b-app/shots/task-09.png
```
Expected: `npm run check` reports 0 errors (`vite.config.js` is type-checked too: the `vitest/config` reference gives it the `test` key and `@types/node` gives it `process` and the request types); `app/src-tauri/icons/` holds the generated set; the build succeeds; the app check passes in well under 2 minutes, `pgrep -x kara-app` and `lsof -ti tcp:1420` then print nothing, and its picture shows a window titled KaraAlwaysOK with the standard macOS title bar, the blue-grey sidebar and the page background. To see that the error check works, add `console.error("check")` to `+page.svelte`'s script, run the app check again (it must fail with a `[webview] console.error: check` line), and remove it. Also start the app check and press Ctrl-C while it waits: afterwards `pgrep -x kara-app` and `lsof -ti tcp:1420` print nothing. If `npm install` reports a peer-dependency conflict, pin the versions from `~/Repos/filmrev/app/package-lock.json` (a known-good Svelte 5 / Kit / Vite 6 / vitest 4 set) instead of loosening ranges.

### Task 10: Six UI languages — keyed text, system language, CJK fonts

**Files:**
- Create: `app/src/lib/i18n/en.ts`, `ja.ts`, `ko.ts`, `zh-Hans.ts`, `zh-Hant.ts`, `es.ts`, `app/src/lib/i18n/index.svelte.ts`, `app/src/lib/i18n/engine.ts`, `app/src/lib/i18n/i18n.test.ts`
- Create: `app/src-tauri/src/settings.rs` (`system_locale`)
- Create: `app/src/lib/api.ts` (typed wrappers over Tauri commands; later tasks append to it)
- Modify: `app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (`sys-locale`), `app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `app/src/routes/+layout.ts`, `app/src/routes/+layout.svelte`, `app/src/styles/tokens.css`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 9 scaffold.
- Produces:
  - Command `system_locale() -> Option<String>` (the macOS preferred language tag, e.g. `"ja-JP"`, `"zh-Hant-TW"`); `api.ts` `systemLocale()`, `ProblemCode` (the Task 8 codes), `AppError { problem: ProblemCode | null; message }` (how every command fails).
  - `$lib/i18n/index.svelte`: `type Key` (every key of `en.ts`), `type Locale = "en" | "ja" | "ko" | "zh-Hans" | "zh-Hant" | "es"`, `DICTS`, `LOCALE_NAMES` (each language in its own script), `matchLocale(tag) -> Locale`, `i18n.{system, choice, locale, init(), pick(choice | null)}`, and `t(key, params?) -> string` — `{name}` placeholders are filled from `params`; when `params.n === 1` and `<key>.one` exists, that text is used.
  - `$lib/i18n/engine`: `say(error) -> string` — an `AppError` (or a failed event) in the current language via its problem code (key `problem.<code>`); one without a code shows its English text; a code this build doesn't know shows `problem.unknown` and never throws. No English text is ever matched.
  - `t()` falls back to English, then to the key itself, so it never throws.
  - Keys `problem.<code>` for every `ProblemCode` (the 29 Task 8 codes, which must stay equal to the Rust enum) plus `problem.unknown`; a missing or extra key in any language is a type error (each locale is a `Record<Key, string>` literal), so no key-parity test is needed.
  - **Rule for every later UI task:** components hold no UI text; every label, tooltip, aria text, toast and message goes through `t()` (or `say()` for messages from Rust), and each task adds its keys to **all six** locale files in the same step. Song titles, artists, albums, playlist names and lyrics are shown exactly as stored — never passed through `t()`.
  - `<html lang>` follows the current locale; CJK text falls back to PingFang / Hiragino Sans / Apple SD Gothic Neo (Traditional Chinese prefers PingFang TC).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`app/src/lib/i18n/i18n.test.ts`:

```ts
import { expect, it } from "vitest";
import { say } from "./engine";
import { matchLocale } from "./index.svelte";

it("follows the system language, English when it isn't one of the six", () => {
  const tags = ["ja-JP", "zh-Hant-TW", "zh-TW", "zh-Hans-CN", "ko-KR", "es-MX", "fr-FR"];
  expect(tags.map(matchLocale)).toEqual(["ja", "zh-Hant", "zh-Hant", "zh-Hans", "ko", "es", "en"]);
});

it("says a problem code it doesn't know as a generic message instead of failing", () => {
  expect(say({ problem: "fromANewerEngine", message: "Something new." })).toBe("Something went wrong.");
  expect(say({ problem: "fileMoved", message: "" })).toBe("The file was moved or deleted.");
  expect(say({ problem: null, message: "Plain text." })).toBe("Plain text.");
});
```

`app/src/lib/api.ts`:

```ts
import { invoke } from "@tauri-apps/api/core";

export type ProblemCode =
  | "newerLibrary" | "dataFolder" | "inUse" | "libraryOpen" | "engineDownload" | "engineStart" | "fileMoved" | "fileNotAllowed"
  | "notAudio" | "songGone" | "noAudio" | "download" | "downloaderSetup" | "unreadable" | "songAudio" | "empty" | "diskFull"
  | "save" | "separate" | "notPrepared" | "partNotReady" | "streamingLater" | "upgradeFailed" | "linkStreaming"
  | "linkUnsupported" | "noSongAtLink" | "readFailed" | "nothingPlaying" | "notALink";

/** How every command fails: a problem code to translate, and the English text. */
export interface AppError { problem: ProblemCode | null; message: string }

export const systemLocale = () => invoke<string | null>("system_locale");
```

`app/src/lib/i18n/index.svelte.ts`:

```ts
import { systemLocale } from "$lib/api";
import en, { type Key } from "./en";
import es from "./es";
import ja from "./ja";
import ko from "./ko";
import zhHans from "./zh-Hans";
import zhHant from "./zh-Hant";

export type { Key };

export const DICTS = { en, ja, ko, "zh-Hans": zhHans, "zh-Hant": zhHant, es };
export type Locale = keyof typeof DICTS;

export const LOCALE_NAMES: Record<Locale, string> = {
  en: "English",
  ja: "日本語",
  ko: "한국어",
  "zh-Hans": "简体中文",
  "zh-Hant": "繁體中文",
  es: "Español",
};

/** The supported locale for a language tag such as "ja-JP" or "zh-Hant-TW"; English when none fits. */
export function matchLocale(tag: string): Locale {
  const t = tag.toLowerCase();
  if (t.startsWith("zh")) return /hant|-tw|-hk|-mo/.test(t) ? "zh-Hant" : "zh-Hans";
  return (["ja", "ko", "es"] as const).find((l) => t.startsWith(l)) ?? "en";
}

function saved(): Locale | null {
  try {
    const v = localStorage.getItem("locale");
    return v && v in DICTS ? (v as Locale) : null;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

class I18n {
  system = $state<Locale>("en");
  /** The language picked in Settings; null follows the system. */
  choice = $state<Locale | null>(saved());
  locale = $derived(this.choice ?? this.system);

  async init() {
    this.system = matchLocale((await systemLocale().catch(() => null)) ?? navigator.language);
  }

  pick(choice: Locale | null) {
    this.choice = choice;
    try {
      if (choice) localStorage.setItem("locale", choice);
      else localStorage.removeItem("locale");
    } catch {
      return;
    }
  }
}

export const i18n = new I18n();

/** UI text for `key` in the current language, with {name} placeholders filled; `<key>.one` is used when n is 1. */
export function t(key: Key, params: Record<string, string | number> = {}): string {
  const dict: Record<string, string> = DICTS[i18n.locale];
  const base: Record<string, string> = DICTS.en;
  const text = (params.n === 1 && (dict[`${key}.one`] ?? base[`${key}.one`])) || dict[key] || base[key] || key;
  return text.replace(/\{(\w+)\}/g, (_, name: string) => String(params[name] ?? ""));
}
```

`app/src/lib/i18n/engine.ts`:

```ts
import type { AppError } from "$lib/api";
import { DICTS, t, type Key } from "./index.svelte";

/** An error from the Rust side in the current language; an unknown code gets a generic text, no code its English text. */
export function say(e: unknown): string {
  const err = e as Partial<AppError> | null;
  if (!err?.problem) return String(err?.message ?? e);
  const key = `problem.${err.problem}`;
  return key in DICTS.en ? t(key as Key) : t("problem.unknown");
}
```
